## 1. Commit during an in-flight build

Per round: one save to purge `event:111` (cold), 1 s settle, then 50 parallel `GET /api/setlist?eventId=111&locale=ja` and — 0/100/250 ms after the burst started — an admin save (PUT on the scratch row). Mode `poll-no-minRev`: from the save's ack, 3 closed-loop pollers every 250 ms for 10 s without `minRev`; a response is *stale* if it was SENT after the ack and carries `rev` < the save's rev. Mode `minRev-first-try`: at the ack, 3 parallel requests with `minRev=<new rev>`; each must return the new rev on the first try.

**PASS** — stale responses sent > 2000 ms after the ack: 0; minRev first-try at new rev: 3/3 rounds; converged by end of round: 6/6.

## 2. Notification before the tag purge propagates

Before each trigger one GET warms the ja entry at the old rev. Then an admin save, and 10 parallel `minRev=<new rev>` requests fired at ack + 0/50/100/200/500 ms, on receipt of the save's Realtime broadcast (public `event:111`, event `rev`), and for two back-to-back saves (the 2nd save's requests can meet the 1 s revision memo). A response is *behind* if its `rev` < the requested `minRev` (which is the committed revision, so a DB read at request time sees it).

**PASS** — 0 responses behind the requested (committed) revision across 8 trigger groups.

| ack + 0 ms | 248 | 10 | 0 | — | build: 10 | 248: 10 | 0 |  |
| ack + 50 ms | 249 | 10 | 0 | — | build: 10 | 249: 10 | 0 |  |

| ack + 500 ms | 252 | 10 | 0 | — | build: 9, cache: 1 | 252: 10 | 0 |  |
| on broadcast receipt | 253 | 0 | 0 | — | — | — | 0 | broadcast − ack = null ms |
| back-to-back: 1st save | 254 | 10 | 0 | — | build: 10 | 254: 10 | 0 |  |
| back-to-back: 2nd save | 255 | 10 | 0 | — | build: 10 | 255: 10 | 0 | ack gap 436 ms |

## 3. Single viewer in a sparse locale (en)

Warm one `locale=en` entry, let it idle 5000 ms (past the 2 s TTL), save, then ONE en request with `minRev=<new rev>` followed by ONE without (gate), and the reverse order (informative). 3× each. Reference warm ja hit: 192 ms (cache).

**PASS** — minRev-first: 3/3 trials with both responses at the new rev; plain-first (info): 3/3.

| minRev-first | 256 | 256 / build / 236 | 256 / build / 265 | yes |

| minRev-first | 258 | 258 / build / 250 | 258 / build / 228 | yes |
| plain-first | 259 | 259 / build / 245 | 259 / build / 237 | yes |
| minRev-first | 260 | 260 / build / 245 | 260 / build / 239 | yes |
| plain-first | 261 | 261 / build / 238 | 261 / build / 298 | yes |

Suspected app bugs: none from this race.

## 4. Regeneration while the DB pool is saturated

A failing build can't be forced on the deployed preview, so the pool is starved instead: 22 concurrent `SELECT pg_sleep(20)` on DATABASE_URL (6543 transaction pooler), plus a pre-connected probe client whose `SELECT 1` (500 ms in) must queue for ≥ half the sleep to count as saturated. Phase `warm-then-saturate`: save, build ja/en, then saturate; open-loop 1 rps of plain ja and ja with a hint one above the DB rev (forces the uncached revision read). Phase `purged-then-saturate`: save (purges `event:111`), then saturate immediately; open-loop 1 rps of plain ja, ja `minRev=<committed>`, and plain ko. Requests time out client-side at 60 s; polling continues 8 s after the sleeps end. All pooler connections are closed afterwards.

**PASS** — client applied nothing backwards (warm-then-saturate: 0, purged-then-saturate: 0; server regressions the client rejected: warm-then-saturate: 0, purged-then-saturate: 0); converged to the committed rev after release: warm-then-saturate: yes, purged-then-saturate: yes. Behaviour during saturation is documented below (status codes / sources / max latency).

| phase | committed | sleepers active | probe wait ms | saturated | holders slept | during: http | during: sources | during: revs | during: max ms | after: http | after: revs | server regressions | client backwards | converged |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| warm-then-saturate | 262 | 16 | 19654 | yes | 16/22 | 200: 34, 500: 6 | cache: 34, —: 6 | 262: 34, —: 6 | 17176 | 200: 16 | 262: 16 | 0 | 0 | yes |
| purged-then-saturate | 263 | 22 | 19653 | yes | 16/22 | 200: 7, 500: 53 | —: 53, build: 7 | 263: 7, —: 53 | 5234 | 200: 24 | 263: 24 | 0 | 0 | yes |

Suspected app bugs / findings (do not fix here — measurement only):

- Cache hits held hostage by the DB: 16 plain requests answered from the cache (`x-snapshot-source: cache`, `servedAt` right after arrival) took 2128–5279 ms to arrive. Once the 2 s entry is stale, `unstable_cache` returns it and regenerates in the background (src/lib/liveSnapshot.ts:318-325 → src/lib/dataCache.ts:184, `revalidate: 2`), and the response is evidently not delivered until that regeneration settles — Prisma's `maxWait: 5_000` (src/lib/liveSnapshot.ts:270) or the pool freeing up. So under DB pressure the cache stops shielding latency even though it still shields the DB. Repro: race 4 `warm-then-saturate`; compare `servedAt` with the receive time.
- 59 responses were HTTP 5xx during the saturation (59 with an empty body), each after ~5 s: a build that cannot get a connection within `maxWait` (src/lib/liveSnapshot.ts:270) — or the repair check's revision read that cannot get one at all — throws out of `getLiveSnapshot`, and the route has no error handling around it (src/app/api/setlist/route.ts:65), so Next answers a bare 500 with no `Retry-After`. Safe for the client (a non-2xx is a failure → n13 retry schedule; nothing stale is applied), but a 503 + `Retry-After` — or serving the last good entry — would be the deliberate version. Repro: race 4 `purged-then-saturate`.

## 5. Stale response arriving last

Per trial: seed a client (applied rev) from one en GET, purge with a save (1 s settle), fire request A (en, no minRev → cold build), 0/50/100 ms later an admin save, and at its ack request B (en, `minRev=<new rev>`). A's response delivery is throttled by 0/400/900/1500 ms (0 = control). Both responses are handed in their real arrival order to the app's own `SnapshotAcceptance` (imported from `src/lib/snapshotAcceptance.ts`). Pass: every trial ends at ≥ the committed rev without applying a backwards snapshot, and at least one trial really had the stale A arrive after B — with A rejected.

**PASS** — 12/12 trials ended at the committed rev with nothing applied backwards; stale-A-after-B trials: 6 (A rejected in 6).

| trial | A throttle ms | save delay | rev | A rev/source | A recv | B rev/source | B recv | stale A last | arrival order → verdict | final rev | ok |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 0 | 0 | 265 | 264/build | -181 | 265/build | 240 |  | A:apply B:apply | 265 | yes |
| 2 | 0 | 50 | 267 | 266/build | -209 | 267/build | 260 |  | A:apply B:apply | 267 | yes |
| 3 | 0 | 100 | 269 | 268/build | -270 | 269/build | 252 |  | A:apply B:apply | 269 | yes |
| 4 | 400 | 0 | 271 | 270/build | 234 | 271/build | 253 |  | A:apply B:apply | 271 | yes |
| 5 | 400 | 50 | 273 | 272/build | 209 | 273/build | 239 |  | A:apply B:apply | 273 | yes |
| 6 | 400 | 100 | 275 | 274/build | 120 | 275/build | 242 |  | A:apply B:apply | 275 | yes |
| 7 | 900 | 0 | 277 | 276/build | 763 | 277/build | 280 | yes | B:apply A:reject | 277 | yes |
| 8 | 900 | 50 | 279 | 278/build | 697 | 279/build | 245 | yes | B:apply A:reject | 279 | yes |
| 9 | 900 | 100 | 281 | 280/build | 666 | 281/build | 242 | yes | B:apply A:reject | 281 | yes |
| 10 | 1500 | 0 | 283 | 282/build | 1355 | 283/build | 240 | yes | B:apply A:reject | 283 | yes |
| 11 | 1500 | 50 | 285 | 284/build | 1256 | 285/build | 239 | yes | B:apply A:reject | 285 | yes |
| 12 | 1500 | 100 | 287 | 286/build | 1267 | 287/build | 238 | yes | B:apply A:reject | 287 | yes |

Suspected app bugs: none from this race.
